   81: =head1 NAME
   82: 
   83: App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor - Extract test schemas from Perl modules
   84: 
   85: =head1 VERSION
   86: 
   87: Version 0.49
   88: 
   89: =cut
   90: 
   91: our $VERSION = '0.49';
   92: 
   93: =head1 SYNOPSIS
   94: 
   95: 	use App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor;
   96: 
   97: 	my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
   98: 		input_file => 'lib/MyModule.pm',
   99: 		output_dir => 'schemas/',
  100: 		verbose	=> 1,
  101: 	);
  102: 
  103: 	my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
  104: 
  105: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  106: 
  107: App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor analyzes Perl modules and generates
  108: structured YAML schema files suitable for automated test generation by L<App::Test::Generator>.
  109: This module employs
  110: static analysis techniques to infer parameter types, constraints, and
  111: method behaviors directly from your source code.
  112: 
  113: =head2 Analysis Methods
  114: 
  115: The extractor combines multiple analysis approaches for a comprehensive schema generation:
  116: 
  117: =over 4
  118: 
  119: =item * B<POD Documentation Analysis>
  120: 
  121: Parses embedded documentation to extract:
  122:   - Parameter names, types, and descriptions from =head2 sections
  123:   - Method signatures with positional parameters
  124:   - Return value specifications from "Returns:" sections
  125:   - Constraints (ranges, patterns, required/optional status)
  126:   - Semantic type detection (email, URL, filename)
  127: 
  128: =item * B<Code Pattern Detection>
  129: 
  130: Analyzes source code using PPI to identify:
  131:   - Method signatures and parameter extraction patterns
  132:   - Type validation (ref(), isa(), blessed())
  133:   - Constraint patterns (length checks, numeric comparisons, regex matches)
  134:   - Return statement analysis and value type inference
  135:   - Object instantiation requirements and accessor methods
  136: 
  137: =item * B<Signature Analysis>
  138: 
  139: Examines method declarations for:
  140:   - Parameter names and positional information
  141:   - Instance vs. class method detection
  142:   - Method modifiers (Moose-style before/after/around)
  143:   - Various parameter declaration styles (shift, @_ assignment)
  144: 
  145: =item * B<Heuristic Inference>
  146: 
  147: Applies Perl-specific domain knowledge:
  148:   - Boolean return detection from method names (is_*, has_*, can_*)
  149:   - Common Perl idioms and coding patterns
  150:   - Context awareness (scalar vs list, wantarray usage)
  151:   - Object-oriented patterns (constructors, accessors, chaining)
  152: 
  153: =back
  154: 
  155: =head2 Generated Schema Structure
  156: 
  157: The extracted schemas follow this YAML structure:
  158: 
  159:     function: method_name
  160:     module: Package::Name
  161:     input:
  162:       param1:
  163:         type: string
  164:         min: 3
  165:         max: 50
  166:         optional: 0
  167:         position: 0
  168:       param2:
  169:         type: integer
  170:         min: 0
  171:         max: 100
  172:         optional: 1
  173:         position: 1
  174:     output:
  175:       type: boolean
  176:       value: 1
  177:     new: Package::Name # if object instantiation required
  178:     config:
  179:       test_empty: 1
  180:       test_nuls: 0
  181:       test_undef: 0
  182:       test_non_ascii: 0
  183: 
  184: =head2 Advanced Detection Capabilities
  185: 
  186: =over 4
  187: 
  188: =item * B<Accessor Method Detection>
  189: 
  190: Automatically identifies getter, setter, and combined accessor methods
  191: by analyzing common patterns like C<return $self-E<gt>{property}> and
  192: C<$self-E<gt>{property} = $value>.
  193: 
  194: =item * B<Params::Get Integration>
  195: 
  196: Recognises parameters extracted via C<Params::Get::get_params('key', \@_)>,
  197: treating the quoted key as a named parameter equivalent to a traditional
  198: C<my ($self, $key) = @_> signature.  This prevents false positives from
  199: C<--strict-pod> when the method body never declares an explicit C<$key>
  200: variable.
  201: 
  202: =item * B<Direct-Index Self Style>
  203: 
  204: Recognises C<my $self = $_[0]> as a valid method-invocant pattern.  Parameters
  205: at C<$_[1]>, C<$_[2]>, etc. are extracted as positional parameters.  Without
  206: this, the signature fallback would incorrectly pick up C<my (...) = @_> from
  207: inner closures defined in the method body and treat those variables as the
  208: outer method's parameters.
  209: 
  210: =item * B<Boolean Return Inference>
  211: 
  212: Detects boolean-returning methods through multiple signals:
  213:   - Method name patterns (is_*, has_*, can_*)
  214:   - Return patterns (consistent 1/0 returns)
  215:   - POD descriptions ("returns true on success")
  216:   - Ternary operators with boolean results
  217: 
  218: =item * B<Context Awareness>
  219: 
  220: Identifies methods that use C<wantarray> and can return different
  221: values in scalar vs list context.
  222: 
  223: =item * B<Object Lifecycle Management>
  224: 
  225: Detects instance methods requiring object instantiation and
  226: automatically adds the C<new> field to schemas.
  227: 
  228: =item * B<Enhanced Object Detection>
  229: 
  230: The extractor includes sophisticated object detection capabilities that go beyond simple instance method identification:
  231: 
  232: =over 4
  233: 
  234: =item * B<Factory Method Recognition>
  235: 
  236: Automatically identifies methods that create and return object instances, such as methods named C<create_*>, C<make_*>, C<build_*>, or C<get_*>. Factory methods are correctly classified as class methods that don't require pre-existing objects for testing.
  237: 
  238: =item * B<Singleton Pattern Detection>
  239: 
  240: Recognizes singleton patterns through multiple signals: method names like C<instance> or C<get_instance>, static variables holding instance references, lazy initialization patterns (C<$instance ||= new()>), and consistent return of the same instance variable.
  241: 
  242: =item * B<Constructor Parameter Analysis>
  243: 
  244: Examines C<new> methods to determine required and optional parameters, validation requirements, and default values. This enables test generators to provide appropriate constructor arguments when object instantiation is needed.
  245: 
  246: =item * B<Inheritance Relationship Handling>
  247: 
  248: Detects parent classes through C<use parent>, C<use base>, and C<@ISA> declarations. Identifies when methods use C<SUPER::> calls and determines whether the current class or a parent class constructor should be used for object instantiation.
  249: 
  250: =item * B<External Object Dependency Detection>
  251: 
  252: Identifies when methods create or depend on objects from other classes, enabling proper test setup with mock objects or real dependencies.
  253: 
  254: =back
  255: 
  256: These enhancements ensure that generated test schemas accurately reflect the object-oriented structure of the code, leading to more meaningful and effective test generation.
  257: 
  258: =back
  259: 
  260: =head2 Confidence Scoring
  261: 
  262: Each generated schema includes detailed confidence assessments:
  263: 
  264: =over 4
  265: 
  266: =item * B<High Confidence>
  267: 
  268: Multiple independent analysis sources converge on consistent,
  269: well-constrained parameters with explicit validation logic and
  270: comprehensive documentation.
  271: 
  272: =item * B<Medium Confidence>
  273: 
  274: Reasonable evidence from code patterns or partial documentation,
  275: but may lack comprehensive constraints or have some ambiguities.
  276: 
  277: =item * B<Low Confidence>
  278: 
  279: Minimal evidence - primarily based on naming conventions,
  280: default assumptions, or single-source analysis.
  281: 
  282: =item * B<Very Low Confidence>
  283: 
  284: Barely any detectable signals - schema should be thoroughly
  285: reviewed before use in test generation.
  286: 
  287: =back
  288: 
  289: =head2 Use Cases
  290: 
  291: =over 4
  292: 
  293: =item * B<Automated Test Generation>
  294: 
  295: Generate comprehensive test suites with L<App::Test::Generator> using
  296: extracted schemas as input. The schemas provide the necessary structure
  297: for generating both positive and negative test cases.
  298: 
  299: =item * B<API Documentation Generation>
  300: 
  301: Supplement existing documentation with automatically inferred interface
  302: specifications, parameter requirements, and return types.
  303: 
  304: =item * B<Code Quality Assessment>
  305: 
  306: Identify methods with poor documentation, inconsistent parameter handling,
  307: or unclear interfaces that may benefit from refactoring.
  308: 
  309: =item * B<Refactoring Assistance>
  310: 
  311: Detect method dependencies, object instantiation requirements, and
  312: parameter usage patterns to inform refactoring decisions.
  313: 
  314: =item * B<Legacy Code Analysis>
  315: 
  316: Quickly understand the interface contracts of legacy Perl codebases
  317: without extensive manual code reading.
  318: 
  319: =back
  320: 
  321: =head2 Integration with Testing Ecosystem
  322: 
  323: The generated schemas are specifically designed to work with the
  324: L<App::Test::Generator> ecosystem:
  325: 
  326:     # Extract schemas from your module
  327:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(...);
  328:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
  329: 
  330:     # Use with test generator (typically as separate steps)
  331:     # fuzz-harness-generator -r schemas/method_name.yml
  332: 
  333: =head2 Limitations and Considerations
  334: 
  335: =over 4
  336: 
  337: =item * B<Dynamic Code Patterns>
  338: 
  339: Highly dynamic code (string evals, AUTOLOAD, symbolic references)
  340: may not be fully detected by static analysis.
  341: 
  342: =item * B<Complex Validation Logic>
  343: 
  344: Sophisticated validation involving multiple parameters or external
  345: dependencies may require manual schema refinement.
  346: 
  347: =item * B<Confidence Heuristics>
  348: 
  349: Confidence scores are based on heuristics and should be reviewed
  350: by developers familiar with the codebase.
  351: 
  352: =item * B<Perl Idiom Recognition>
  353: 
  354: Some Perl-specific idioms may require custom pattern recognition
  355: beyond the built-in detectors.
  356: 
  357: =item * B<Documentation Dependency>
  358: 
  359: Analysis quality improves significantly with comprehensive POD
  360: documentation following consistent patterns.
  361: 
  362: =item * B<Multi-Package Files>
  363: 
  364: Only the first package in a file is analysed.  A file that declares
  365: additional packages, such as a small error class, is still extracted, but
  366: its schemas carry a C<skip> reason so generated harnesses are not run
  367: against it.
  368: 
  369: =back
  370: 
  371: =head2 Best Practices for Optimal Results
  372: 
  373: =over 4
  374: 
  375: =item * B<Comprehensive POD Documentation>
  376: 
  377: Write detailed POD with explicit parameter documentation using
  378: consistent patterns like C<$param - type (constraints), description>.
  379: 
  380: =item * B<Consistent Coding Patterns>
  381: 
  382: Use consistent parameter validation patterns and method signatures
  383: throughout your codebase.
  384: 
  385: =item * B<Schema Review Process>
  386: 
  387: Review and refine automatically generated schemas, particularly
  388: those with low confidence scores.
  389: 
  390: =item * B<Descriptive Naming>
  391: 
  392: Use descriptive method and parameter names that clearly indicate
  393: purpose and expected types.
  394: 
  395: =item * B<Progressive Enhancement>
  396: 
  397: Start with automatically generated schemas and progressively
  398: refine them based on test results and code understanding.
  399: 
  400: =back
  401: 
  402: The module is particularly valuable for large codebases where manual schema
  403: creation would be prohibitively time-consuming, and for maintaining test
  404: coverage as code evolves through continuous integration pipelines.
  405: 
  406: =head2 Advanced Type Detection
  407: 
  408: The schema extractor includes enhanced type detection capabilities that identify specialized Perl types beyond basic strings and integers.
  409: L<DateTime> and L<Time::Piece> objects are detected through isa() checks and method call patterns, while date strings (ISO 8601, YYYY-MM-DD) and UNIX timestamps are recognized through regex validation and numeric range checks.
  410: File handles and file paths are identified via I/O operations and file test operators, coderefs are detected through ref() checks and invocation patterns, and enum-like parameters are extracted from validation code including regex patterns (C</^(a|b|c)$/>), hash lookups, grep statements, and if/elsif chains.
  411: These detected types are preserved in the generated YAML schemas with appropriate semantic annotations, enabling test generators to create more accurate and meaningful test cases.
  412: 
  413: =head3 Example Advanced Type Schema
  414: 
  415: For a method like:
  416: 
  417:     sub process_event {
  418:         my ($self, $timestamp, $status, $callback) = @_;
  419:         croak unless $timestamp > 1000000000;
  420:         croak unless $status =~ /^(active|pending|complete)$/;
  421:         croak unless ref($callback) eq 'CODE';
  422:         $callback->($timestamp, $status);
  423:     }
  424: 
  425: The extractor generates:
  426: 
  427:     ---
  428:     function: process_event
  429:     module: MyModule
  430:     input:
  431:       timestamp:
  432:         type: integer
  433:         # min: 0
  434:         # max: 2147483647
  435:         position: 0
  436:         _note: Unix timestamp
  437: 	semantic: unix_timestamp
  438:       status:
  439:         type: string
  440:         enum:
  441:           - active
  442:           - pending
  443:           - complete
  444:         position: 1
  445:         _note: 'Must be one of: active, pending, complete'
  446:       callback:
  447:         type: coderef
  448:         position: 2
  449:         _note: 'CODE reference - provide sub { } in tests'
  450: 
  451: =head1 RELATIONSHIP DETECTION
  452: 
  453: The schema extractor detects relationships and dependencies between parameters,
  454: enabling more sophisticated validation and test generation.
  455: 
  456: =head2 Relationship Types
  457: 
  458: =over 4
  459: 
  460: =item * B<mutually_exclusive>
  461: 
  462: Parameters that cannot be used together.
  463: 
  464:     die if $file && $content;  # Can't specify both
  465: 
  466: Generated schema:
  467: 
  468:     relationships:
  469:       - type: mutually_exclusive
  470:         params: [file, content]
  471:         description: Cannot specify both file and content
  472: 
  473: =item * B<required_group>
  474: 
  475: At least one parameter from the group must be specified (OR logic).
  476: 
  477:     die unless $id || $name;  # Must provide one
  478: 
  479: Generated schema:
  480: 
  481:     relationships:
  482:       - type: required_group
  483:         params: [id, name]
  484:         logic: or
  485:         description: Must specify either id or name
  486: 
  487: =item * B<conditional_requirement>
  488: 
  489: If one parameter is specified, another becomes required (IF-THEN logic).
  490: 
  491:     die if $async && !$callback;  # async requires callback
  492: 
  493: Generated schema:
  494: 
  495:     relationships:
  496:       - type: conditional_requirement
  497:         if: async
  498:         then_required: callback
  499:         description: When async is specified, callback is required
  500: 
  501: =item * B<dependency>
  502: 
  503: One parameter depends on another being present.
  504: 
  505:     die "Port requires host" if $port && !$host;
  506: 
  507: Generated schema:
  508: 
  509:     relationships:
  510:       - type: dependency
  511:         param: port
  512:         requires: host
  513:         description: port requires host to be specified
  514: 
  515: =item * B<value_constraint>
  516: 
  517: Specific value requirements between parameters.
  518: 
  519:     die if $ssl && $port != 443;  # ssl requires port 443
  520: 
  521: Generated schema:
  522: 
  523:     relationships:
  524:       - type: value_constraint
  525:         if: ssl
  526:         then: port
  527:         operator: ==
  528:         value: 443
  529:         description: When ssl is specified, port must equal 443
  530: 
  531: =item * B<value_conditional>
  532: 
  533: Parameter required when another has a specific value.
  534: 
  535:     die if $mode eq 'secure' && !$key;
  536: 
  537: Generated schema:
  538: 
  539:     relationships:
  540:       - type: value_conditional
  541:         if: mode
  542:         equals: secure
  543:         then_required: key
  544:         description: When mode equals 'secure', key is required
  545: 
  546: =back
  547: 
  548: =head2 Default Value Extraction
  549: 
  550: The extractor comprehensively extracts default values from both code and POD documentation:
  551: 
  552: =head3 Code Pattern Recognition
  553: 
  554: Extracts defaults from multiple Perl idioms:
  555: 
  556: =over 4
  557: 
  558: =item * Logical OR operator: C<$param = $param || 'default'>
  559: 
  560: =item * Defined-or operator: C<$param //= 'default'>
  561: 
  562: =item * Ternary operator: C<$param = defined $param ? $param : 'default'>
  563: 
  564: =item * Unless conditional: C<$param = 'default' unless defined $param>
  565: 
  566: =item * Chained defaults: C<$param = $param || $self->{_default} || 'fallback'>
  567: 
  568: =item * Multi-line patterns: C<$param = {} unless $param>
  569: 
  570: =back
  571: 
  572: =head3 POD Pattern Recognition
  573: 
  574: Extracts defaults from documentation:
  575: 
  576: =over 4
  577: 
  578: =item * Standard format: C<Default: 'value'>
  579: 
  580: =item * Alternative format: C<Defaults to: 'value'>
  581: 
  582: =item * Inline format: C<Optional, default: 'value'>
  583: 
  584: =item * Parameter lists: C<$param - type, default 'value'>
  585: 
  586: =back
  587: 
  588: =head3 Value Processing
  589: 
  590: Properly handles:
  591: 
  592: =over 4
  593: 
  594: =item * String literals with quotes and escape sequences
  595: 
  596: =item * Numeric values (integers and floats)
  597: 
  598: =item * Boolean values (true/false converted to 1/0)
  599: 
  600: =item * Empty data structures ([] and {})
  601: 
  602: =item * Special values (undef, __PACKAGE__)
  603: 
  604: =item * Complex expressions (preserved as-is when unevaluatable)
  605: 
  606: =item * Quote operators (q{}, qq{}, qw{})
  607: 
  608: =back
  609: 
  610: =head3 Type Inference
  611: 
  612: When a parameter has a default value but no explicit type annotation,
  613: the type is automatically inferred from the default:
  614: 
  615:     $options = {}        # inferred as hashref
  616:     $items = []          # inferred as arrayref
  617:     $count = 42          # inferred as integer
  618:     $ratio = 3.14        # inferred as number
  619:     $enabled = 1         # inferred as boolean
  620: 
  621: =head2 Context-Aware Return Analysis
  622: 
  623: The extractor provides comprehensive analysis of method return behavior,
  624: including context sensitivity, error handling conventions, and method chaining patterns.
  625: 
  626: When a method's POD contains a C<=head4 Output> block in
  627: L<Params::Validate::Strict> schema format, the C<type> declared there is
  628: used as the authoritative output type and takes precedence over all
  629: heuristic code analysis:
  630: 
  631:     =head4 Output
  632: 
  633:         {
  634:             type => 'hashref',
  635:         }
  636: 
  637: This is the recommended way to document methods whose return type would
  638: otherwise be misidentified (e.g. a method that returns C<$self-E<gt>{cache}>
  639: where the cache happens to hold a hashref).
  640: 
  641: Using parentheses as the outer container emits C<type: array>, indicating a
  642: list-returning method.  L<App::Test::Generator> 0.39+ (with L<Test::Returns>
  643: 0.03+) captures these results in list context automatically:
  644: 
  645:     =head4 Output
  646: 
  647:         (
  648:             {
  649:                 type => 'hashref',
  650:             },
  651:             ...
  652:         )
  653: 
  654: =head3 List vs Scalar Context Detection
  655: 
  656: Automatically detects methods that return different values based on calling context:
  657: 
  658:     sub get_items {
  659:         my $self = $_[0];
  660:         return wantarray ? @items : scalar(@items);
  661:     }
  662: 
  663: Detection captures:
  664: 
  665: =over 4
  666: 
  667: =item * C<_context_aware> flag - Method uses wantarray
  668: 
  669: =item * C<_list_context> - Type returned in list context (e.g., 'array')
  670: 
  671: =item * C<_scalar_context> - Type returned in scalar context (e.g., 'integer')
  672: 
  673: =back
  674: 
  675: Recognizes both ternary operator patterns and conditional return patterns.
  676: 
  677: =head3 Void Context Methods
  678: 
  679: Identifies methods that don't return meaningful values:
  680: 
  681: =over 4
  682: 
  683: =item * Setters (C<set_*> methods)
  684: 
  685: =item * Mutators (C<add_*, remove_*, delete_*, clear_*, reset_*, update_*>)
  686: 
  687: =item * Loggers (C<log, debug, warn, error, info>)
  688: 
  689: =item * Methods with only empty returns
  690: 
  691: =back
  692: 
  693: Example:
  694: 
  695:     sub set_name {
  696:         my ($self, $name) = @_;
  697:         $self->{name} = $name;
  698:         return;  # Void context
  699:     }
  700: 
  701: Sets C<_void_context> flag and C<type =E<gt> 'void'>.
  702: 
  703: =head3 Method Chaining Detection
  704: 
  705: Identifies chainable methods that return C<$self> for fluent interfaces:
  706: 
  707:     sub set_width {
  708:         my ($self, $width) = @_;
  709:         $self->{width} = $width;
  710:         return $self;  # Chainable
  711:     }
  712: 
  713: Detection provides:
  714: 
  715: =over 4
  716: 
  717: =item * C<_returns_self> - Returns invocant for chaining
  718: 
  719: =item * C<class> - The class name being returned
  720: 
  721: =back
  722: 
  723: Also detects chaining documentation in POD (keywords: "chainable", "fluent interface",
  724: "returns self", "method chaining").
  725: 
  726: =head3 Error Return Conventions
  727: 
  728: Analyzes how methods signal errors:
  729: 
  730: B<Pattern Detection:>
  731: 
  732: =over 4
  733: 
  734: =item * C<undef_on_error> - Explicit C<return undef if/unless condition>
  735: 
  736: =item * C<implicit_undef> - Bare C<return if/unless condition>
  737: 
  738: =item * C<empty_list> - C<return ()> for list context errors
  739: 
  740: =item * C<zero_on_error> - Returns 0/false for boolean error indication
  741: 
  742: =item * C<exception_handling> - Uses eval blocks with error checking
  743: 
  744: =back
  745: 
  746: B<Example Analysis:>
  747: 
  748:     sub fetch_user {
  749:         my ($self, $id) = @_;
  750: 
  751:         return undef unless $id;        # undef_on_error
  752:         return undef if $id < 0;        # undef_on_error
  753: 
  754:         return $self->{users}{$id};
  755:     }
  756: 
  757: Results in:
  758: 
  759:     _error_return: 'undef'
  760:     _success_failure_pattern: 1
  761:     _error_handling: {
  762:         undef_on_error: ['$id', '$id < 0']
  763:     }
  764: 
  765: B<Success/Failure Pattern:>
  766: 
  767: Methods that return different types for success vs. failure are flagged with
  768: C<_success_failure_pattern>. Common patterns:
  769: 
  770: =over 4
  771: 
  772: =item * Returns value on success, undef on failure
  773: 
  774: =item * Returns true on success, false on failure
  775: 
  776: =item * Returns data on success, empty list on failure
  777: 
  778: =back
  779: 
  780: =head3 Success Indicator Detection
  781: 
  782: Methods that always return true (typically for side effects):
  783: 
  784:     sub update_status {
  785:         my ($self, $status) = @_;
  786:         $self->{status} = $status;
  787:         return 1;  # Success indicator
  788:     }
  789: 
  790: Sets C<_success_indicator> flag when method consistently returns 1.
  791: 
  792: =head3 Schema Output
  793: 
  794: Enhanced return analysis adds these fields to method schemas:
  795: 
  796:     output:
  797:       type: boolean              # Inferred return type
  798:       _context_aware: 1           # Uses wantarray
  799:       _list_context:
  800:         type: array
  801:       _scalar_context:
  802:         type: integer
  803:       _returns_self: 1               # Returns $self
  804:       _void_context: 1            # No meaningful return
  805:       _success_indicator: 1       # Always returns true
  806:       _error_return: undef        # How errors are signaled
  807:       _success_failure_pattern: 1 # Mixed return types
  808:       _error_handling:            # Detailed error patterns
  809:         undef_on_error: [...]
  810:         exception_handling: 1
  811: 
  812: This comprehensive analysis enables:
  813: 
  814: =over 4
  815: 
  816: =item * Better test generation (testing both contexts, error paths)
  817: 
  818: =item * Documentation generation (clear error conventions)
  819: 
  820: =item * API design validation (consistent error handling)
  821: 
  822: =item * Contract specification (precise return behavior)
  823: 
  824: =back
  825: 
  826: =head2 Example
  827: 
  828: For a method like:
  829: 
  830:     sub connect {
  831:         my ($self, $host, $port, $ssl, $file, $content) = @_;
  832: 
  833:         die if $file && $content;                    # mutually exclusive
  834:         die unless $host || $file;                   # required group
  835:         die "Port requires host" if $port && !$host; # dependency
  836:         die if $ssl && $port != 443;                 # value constraint
  837: 
  838:         # ... connection logic
  839:     }
  840: 
  841: The extractor generates:
  842: 
  843:     relationships:
  844:       - type: mutually_exclusive
  845:         params: [file, content]
  846:         description: Cannot specify both file and content
  847:       - type: required_group
  848:         params: [host, file]
  849:         logic: or
  850:         description: Must specify either host or file
  851:       - type: dependency
  852:         param: port
  853:         requires: host
  854:         description: port requires host to be specified
  855:       - type: value_constraint
  856:         if: ssl
  857:         then: port
  858:         operator: ==
  859:         value: 443
  860:         description: When ssl is specified, port must equal 443
  861: 
  862: =head1 MODERN PERL FEATURES
  863: 
  864: This module adds support for:
  865: 
  866: =head2 Subroutine Signatures (Perl 5.20+)
  867: 
  868:     sub connect($host, $port = 3306, %options) {
  869:         ...
  870:     }
  871: 
  872: Extracts: required params, optional params with defaults, slurpy params
  873: 
  874: =head2 Type Constraints (Perl 5.36+)
  875: 
  876:     sub calculate($x :Int, $y :Num) {
  877:         ...
  878:     }
  879: 
  880: Recognizes: Int, Num, Str, Bool, ArrayRef, HashRef, custom classes
  881: 
  882: =head3 Subroutine Attributes
  883: 
  884:     sub get_value :lvalue :Returns(Int) {
  885:         ...
  886:     }
  887: 
  888: Detects: :lvalue, :method, :Returns(Type), custom attributes
  889: 
  890: =head2 Postfix Dereferencing (Perl 5.20+)
  891: 
  892:     my @array = $arrayref->@*;
  893:     my %hash = $hashref->%*;
  894:     my @slice = $arrayref->@[1,3,5];
  895: 
  896: Tracks usage of modern dereferencing syntax
  897: 
  898: =head2 Field Declarations (Perl 5.38+)
  899: 
  900:     field $host :param = 'localhost';
  901:     field $port :param(port_number) = 3306;
  902:     field $logger :param :isa(Log::Any);
  903: 
  904: Extracts fields and maps them to parameters
  905: 
  906: =head2 Modern Perl Features Support
  907: 
  908: The schema extractor supports modern Perl syntax introduced in versions 5.20, 5.36, and 5.38+.
  909: 
  910: =head3 Subroutine Signatures (Perl 5.20+)
  911: 
  912: Automatically extracts parameters from native Perl signatures:
  913: 
  914:     use feature 'signatures';
  915: 
  916:     sub connect($host, $port = 3306, $database = undef) {
  917:         ...
  918:     }
  919: 
  920: Extracted schema includes:
  921: 
  922: =over 4
  923: 
  924: =item * Parameter positions
  925: 
  926: =item * Optional vs required parameters
  927: 
  928: =item * Default values from signature
  929: 
  930: =item * Slurpy parameters (@array, %hash)
  931: 
  932: =back
  933: 
  934: B<Example:>
  935: 
  936:     # Signature with defaults
  937:     sub process($file, %options) { ... }
  938: 
  939:     # Extracts:
  940:     # $file: position 0, required
  941:     # %options: position 1, optional, slurpy hash
  942: 
  943: =head3 Type Constraints in Signatures (Perl 5.36+)
  944: 
  945: Recognizes type constraints in signature parameters:
  946: 
  947:     sub calculate($x :Int, $y :Num, $name :Str = "result") {
  948:         return $x + $y;
  949:     }
  950: 
  951: Supported constraint types:
  952: 
  953: =over 4
  954: 
  955: =item * C<:Int, :Integer> -> integer
  956: 
  957: =item * C<:Num, :Number> -> number
  958: 
  959: =item * C<:Str, :String> -> string
  960: 
  961: =item * C<:Bool, :Boolean> -> boolean
  962: 
  963: =item * C<:ArrayRef, :Array> -> arrayref
  964: 
  965: =item * C<:HashRef, :Hash> -> hashref
  966: 
  967: =item * C<:ClassName> -> object with isa constraint
  968: 
  969: =back
  970: 
  971: Type constraints are combined with defaults when both are present.
  972: 
  973: =head3 Subroutine Attributes
  974: 
  975: Extracts and documents subroutine attributes:
  976: 
  977:     sub get_value :lvalue {
  978:         my $self = shift;
  979:         return $self->{value};
  980:     }
  981: 
  982:     sub calculate :Returns(Int) :method {
  983:         my ($self, $x, $y) = @_;
  984:         return $x + $y;
  985:     }
  986: 
  987: Recognized attributes stored in C<_attributes> field:
  988: 
  989: =over 4
  990: 
  991: =item * C<:lvalue> - Method can be assigned to
  992: 
  993: =item * C<:method> - Explicitly marked as method
  994: 
  995: =item * C<:Returns(Type)> - Declares return type
  996: 
  997: =item * Custom attributes with values: C<:MyAttr(value)>
  998: 
  999: =back
 1000: 
 1001: =head3 Postfix Dereferencing (Perl 5.20+)
 1002: 
 1003: Detects usage of postfix dereferencing syntax:
 1004: 
 1005:     use feature 'postderef';
 1006: 
 1007:     sub process_array {
 1008:         my ($self, $arrayref) = @_;
 1009:         my @array = $arrayref->@*;        # Array dereference
 1010:         my @slice = $arrayref->@[1,3,5];  # Array slice
 1011:         return @array;
 1012:     }
 1013: 
 1014:     sub process_hash {
 1015:         my ($self, $hashref) = @_;
 1016:         my %hash = $hashref->%*;          # Hash dereference
 1017:         return keys %hash;
 1018:     }
 1019: 
 1020: Tracked features stored in C<_modern_features>:
 1021: 
 1022: =over 4
 1023: 
 1024: =item * C<array_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>@*>
 1025: 
 1026: =item * C<hash_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>%*>
 1027: 
 1028: =item * C<scalar_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>$*>
 1029: 
 1030: =item * C<code_deref> - Uses C<-E<gt>&*>
 1031: 
 1032: =item * C<array_slice> - Uses C<-E<gt>@[...]>
 1033: 
 1034: =item * C<hash_slice> - Uses C<-E<gt>%{...}>
 1035: 
 1036: =back
 1037: 
 1038: =head3 Field Declarations (Perl 5.38+)
 1039: 
 1040: Extracts field declarations from class syntax and maps them to method parameters:
 1041: 
 1042:     use feature 'class';
 1043: 
 1044:     class DatabaseConnection {
 1045:         field $host :param = 'localhost';
 1046:         field $port :param = 3306;
 1047:         field $username :param(user);
 1048:         field $password :param;
 1049:         field $logger :param :isa(Log::Any);
 1050: 
 1051:         method connect() {
 1052:             # Fields available as instance variables
 1053:         }
 1054:     }
 1055: 
 1056: Field attributes:
 1057: 
 1058: =over 4
 1059: 
 1060: =item * C<:param> - Field is a constructor parameter (uses field name)
 1061: 
 1062: =item * C<:param(name)> - Field maps to parameter with different name
 1063: 
 1064: =item * C<:isa(Class)> - Type constraint for the field
 1065: 
 1066: =item * Default values in field declarations
 1067: 
 1068: =back
 1069: 
 1070: Extracted schema includes both field information in C<_fields> and merged parameter
 1071: information in C<input>, allowing proper validation of class constructors.
 1072: 
 1073: =head3 Mixed Modern and Traditional Syntax
 1074: 
 1075: The extractor handles code that mixes modern and traditional syntax:
 1076: 
 1077:     sub modern($x, $y = 5) {
 1078:         # Modern signature with default
 1079:     }
 1080: 
 1081:     sub traditional {
 1082:         my ($self, $x, $y) = @_;
 1083:         $y //= 5;  # Traditional default in code
 1084:         # Both extract same parameter information
 1085:     }
 1086: 
 1087: Priority order for parameter information:
 1088: 
 1089: =over 4
 1090: 
 1091: =item 1. Signature declarations (highest priority)
 1092: 
 1093: =item 2. Field declarations (for class methods)
 1094: 
 1095: =item 3. POD documentation
 1096: 
 1097: =item 4. Code analysis (lowest priority)
 1098: 
 1099: =back
 1100: 
 1101: This ensures that explicit declarations in signatures take precedence over
 1102: inferred information from code analysis.
 1103: 
 1104: =head3 Backwards Compatibility
 1105: 
 1106: All modern Perl feature detection is optional and automatic:
 1107: 
 1108: =over 4
 1109: 
 1110: =item * Traditional C<sub> declarations continue to work
 1111: 
 1112: =item * Code without modern features extracts parameters as before
 1113: 
 1114: =item * Modern features are additive - they enhance rather than replace existing extraction
 1115: 
 1116: =item * Each parameter carries one C<_source> value -- C<validator>, C<input_spec>, C<pod>, C<code>, C<signature> or C<field> -- which the harness uses for its type-checking decisions
 1117: 
 1118: =item * Each constraint that can come from more than one place also carries its own provenance in C<_constraint_source>, a hashref keyed by C<min>, C<max>, C<memberof>, C<matches>, C<nomatch> and C<notmemberof>.  A minimum documented in POD and a maximum enforced by the code on the same field therefore stay separate decisions: the documented one is a hint for valid data, the enforced one is a check
 1119: 
 1120: =back
 1121: 
 1122: =head2 _yamltest_hints
 1123: 
 1124: Each method schema returned by L</extract_all> now optionally includes a
 1125: C<_yamltest_hints> key, which provides guidance for automated test generation
 1126: based on the code analysis.
 1127: 
 1128: This is intended to help L<App::Test::Generator> create meaningful tests,
 1129: including boundary and invalid input cases, without manually specifying them.
 1130: 
 1131: The structure is a hashref with the following keys:
 1132: 
 1133: =over 4
 1134: 
 1135: =item * boundary_values
 1136: 
 1137: An arrayref of numeric values that represent boundaries detected from
 1138: comparisons in the code. These are derived from literals in statements
 1139: like C<$x < 0> or C<$y >= 255>. The generator can use these to create
 1140: boundary tests.
 1141: 
 1142: Example:
 1143: 
 1144:     _yamltest_hints:
 1145:       boundary_values: [0, 1, 100, 255]
 1146: 
 1147: =item * invalid_inputs
 1148: 
 1149: An arrayref of values that are likely to be rejected by the method,
 1150: based on checks like C<defined>, empty strings, or numeric validations.
 1151: 
 1152: Example:
 1153: 
 1154:     _yamltest_hints:
 1155:       invalid_inputs: [undef, '', -1]
 1156: 
 1157: =item * equivalence_classes
 1158: 
 1159: An arrayref intended to capture detected equivalence classes or patterns
 1160: among inputs. Currently this is empty by default, but future enhancements
 1161: may populate it based on detected input groupings.
 1162: 
 1163: Example:
 1164: 
 1165:     _yamltest_hints:
 1166:       equivalence_classes: []
 1167: 
 1168: =back
 1169: 
 1170: =head3 Usage
 1171: 
 1172: When calling C<extract_all>, each method schema will include
 1173: C<_yamltest_hints> if any hints were detected:
 1174: 
 1175:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all;
 1176:     my $hints  = $schemas->{example_method}->{_yamltest_hints};
 1177: 
 1178: You can then feed these hints into automated test generators to produce
 1179: negative tests, boundary tests, and parameter-specific test cases.
 1180: 
 1181: =head3 Notes
 1182: 
 1183: =over 4
 1184: 
 1185: =item * Hints are inferred heuristically from code and validation statements.
 1186: 
 1187: =item * Not all inputs are guaranteed to be detected; the feature is additive
 1188: and will never remove information from the schema.
 1189: 
 1190: =item * Currently, equivalence classes are not populated, but the field exists
 1191: for future extension.
 1192: 
 1193: =item * Boundary and invalid input hints are deduplicated to avoid repeated
 1194: test values.
 1195: 
 1196: =back
 1197: 
 1198: =head3 Examples
 1199: 
 1200: Given a method like:
 1201: 
 1202:     sub example {
 1203:         my ($x) = @_;
 1204:         die "negative" if $x < 0;
 1205:         return unless defined($x);
 1206:         return $x * 2;
 1207:     }
 1208: 
 1209: After running:
 1210: 
 1211:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
 1212:         input_file => 'TestHints.pm',
 1213:         output_dir => '/tmp',
 1214:         quiet    => 1,
 1215:     );
 1216: 
 1217:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all;
 1218: 
 1219: The schema for the method "example" will include:
 1220: 
 1221:     $schemas->{example} = {
 1222:         function => 'example',
 1223:         _confidence => {
 1224:             input  => 'unknown',
 1225:             output => 'unknown',
 1226:         },
 1227:         input => {
 1228:             x => {
 1229:                 type     => 'scalar',
 1230:                 optional => 0,
 1231:             }
 1232:         },
 1233:         output => {
 1234:             type => 'scalar',
 1235:         },
 1236:         _yamltest_hints => {
 1237:             boundary_values => [0, 1],
 1238:             invalid_inputs  => [undef, -1],
 1239:             equivalence_classes => [],
 1240:         },
 1241:         _notes => '...',
 1242:         _analysis => {
 1243:             input_confidence  => 'low',
 1244:             output_confidence => 'unknown',
 1245:             confidence_factors => {
 1246:                 input  => {...},
 1247:                 output => {...},
 1248:             },
 1249:             overall_confidence => 'low',
 1250:         },
 1251:         _fields => {},
 1252:         _modern_features => {},
 1253:         _attributes => {},
 1254:     };
 1255: 
 1256: =head1 METHODS
 1257: 
 1258: =head2 new
 1259: 
 1260: Construct a new SchemaExtractor for a given Perl source file.
 1261: 
 1262:     my $extractor = App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor->new(
 1263:         input_file           => 'lib/MyModule.pm',  # Required
 1264:         output_dir           => 'schemas/',         # Optional - only needed if writing schemas
 1265:         verbose              => 1,                  # Default: 0
 1266:         include_private      => 1,                  # Default: 0
 1267:         max_parameters       => 50,                 # Default: 20
 1268:         confidence_threshold => 0.7,               # Default: 0.5
 1269:         strict_pod           => 0|1|2,              # Default: 0 (off)
 1270:         allow_signature_exec => 1,                  # Default: 0 (off)
 1271:     );
 1272: 
 1273: =head3 Arguments
 1274: 
 1275: =over 4
 1276: 
 1277: =item * C<$input_file>
 1278: 
 1279: Path to the Perl source file to analyse. Required. Must exist on disk.
 1280: 
 1281: =item * C<output_dir>
 1282: 
 1283: Directory to write generated schema YAML files. Optional - only
 1284: required if C<_write_schema> will be called. Callers passing
 1285: C<no_write =E<gt> 1> to C<extract_all> do not need to supply it.
 1286: 
 1287: =item * C<verbose>
 1288: 
 1289: Print progress messages to stdout during analysis. Optional, default 0.
 1290: 
 1291: =item * C<include_private>
 1292: 
 1293: Include methods whose names begin with C<_> in the analysis. Optional,
 1294: default 0. Methods whose name begins with C<_new>, C<_init>, or
 1295: C<_build> are always included regardless of this setting (a prefix
 1296: match, so e.g. C<_build_attribute> and C<_init_logger> qualify too,
 1297: matching common Moose builder/initializer naming conventions).
 1298: 
 1299: =item * C<max_parameters>
 1300: 
 1301: Safety limit on the number of parameters analysed per method to prevent
 1302: runaway processing on pathological code. Optional, default 20.
 1303: 
 1304: =item * C<confidence_threshold>
 1305: 
 1306: Minimum confidence score (0.0-1.0) below which a schema is marked with
 1307: C<_low_confidence =E<gt> 1>. Optional, default 0.5.
 1308: 
 1309: =item * C<strict_pod>
 1310: 
 1311: Controls POD/code agreement validation. C<0> disables validation,
 1312: C<1> emits warnings, C<2> croaks on first disagreement. Also accepts
 1313: the strings C<off>, C<warn>, and C<fatal>. Optional, default 0.
 1314: 
 1315: =item * C<allow_signature_exec>
 1316: 
 1317: Opt-in flag allowing extraction of parameter types from a
 1318: L<Type::Params> C<signature_for()> declaration. This requires actually
 1319: running the C<signature_for> expression (sliced from the target
 1320: module's own source) in a forked C<perl -T> process, since
 1321: L<Type::Params> types are runtime objects that cannot be introspected
 1322: statically. Every other extraction path in this module is static
 1323: (L<PPI>-only) analysis that never executes any of the target module's
 1324: code; this is the one exception. Optional, default 0 (the
 1325: C<signature_for> path is silently skipped, with a warning under
 1326: C<verbose>, when off). Only enable this for modules whose code you
 1327: already trust enough to execute.
 1328: 
 1329: =back
 1330: 
 1331: =head3 Returns
 1332: 
 1333: A blessed hashref. Croaks if C<input_file> is missing or does not
 1334: exist on disk.
 1335: 
 1336: =head3 Side effects
 1337: 
 1338: Reads and parses the input file using L<PPI> at construction time.
 1339: 
 1340: =head3 API specification
 1341: 
 1342: =head4 input
 1343: 
 1344:     {
 1345:         input_file           => { type => SCALAR },
 1346:         output_dir           => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1347:         verbose              => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1348:         include_private      => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1349:         max_parameters       => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1350:         confidence_threshold => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1351:         strict_pod           => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1352:         allow_signature_exec => { type => SCALAR,  optional => 1 },
 1353:     }
 1354: 
 1355: =head4 output
 1356: 
 1357:     {
 1358:         type => OBJECT,

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 1359:         isa  => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor',
 1360:     }
 1361: 
 1362: =cut

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1363:

 1376: 		output_dir => $params->{output_dir},
 1377: 		verbose	=> $params->{verbose} // 0,
 1378: 		include_private => $params->{include_private} // 0,	# include _private methods
 1379: 		confidence_threshold => $params->{confidence_threshold} // $DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE_THRESH,
 1380: 		max_parameters       => $params->{max_parameters}       // $DEFAULT_MAX_PARAMETERS,	# safety limit
 1381: 		strict_pod => _validate_strictness_level($params->{strict_pod}),	# Enable strict POD checking
 1382: 		allow_signature_exec => $params->{allow_signature_exec} // 0,	# opt-in: execute Type::Params signature_for() exprs from the target module
 1383: 	};
 1384: 
 1385: 	# Validate input file exists
 1386: 	unless (-f $self->{input_file}) {
 1387: 		croak(__PACKAGE__, ": Input file '$self->{input_file}' does not exist");
 1388: 	}
 1389: 
 1390: 	return bless $self, $class;
 1391: }
 1392: 
 1393: =head2 extract_all
 1394: 
 1395: Extract schemas for all qualifying methods in the module and return
 1396: them as a hashref.
 1397: 
 1398:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all();
 1399: 
 1400:     # Suppress writing .yml files to disk
 1401:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all(no_write => 1);
 1402: 
 1403: =head3 Arguments
 1404: 
 1405: =over 4
 1406: 
 1407: =item * C<no_write>
 1408: 
 1409: When true, schema files are not written to C<output_dir>. The returned
 1410: hashref is still fully populated. Useful when the caller wants to
 1411: inspect or augment schemas before deciding whether to write them.
 1412: Optional, default 0.
 1413: 
 1414: =back
 1415: 
 1416: =head3 Returns
 1417: 
 1418: A hashref mapping method name strings to schema hashrefs. Each schema
 1419: contains at minimum the keys C<function>, C<module>, C<input>,
 1420: C<output>, and C<_analysis>. See L</Generated Schema Structure> for
 1421: the full structure.
 1422: 
 1423: =head3 Side effects
 1424: 
 1425: Parses the input file with L<PPI>. Writes one YAML file per method to
 1426: C<output_dir> unless C<no_write> is set. Creates C<output_dir> if it
 1427: does not exist and writing is enabled.
 1428: 
 1429: =head3 Notes
 1430: 
 1431: Private methods (names beginning with C<_>) are excluded unless
 1432: C<include_private =E<gt> 1> was passed to C<new>. Duplicate method
 1433: names are deduplicated with a warning logged to stdout in verbose mode.
 1434: 
 1435: POD/code agreement validation is applied if C<strict_pod> was set in
 1436: C<new>. At level 2 (fatal), the first disagreement causes an immediate
 1437: croak.
 1438: 
 1439: =head3 API specification
 1440: 
 1441: =head4 input
 1442: 
 1443:     {
 1444:         self     => { type => OBJECT, isa => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor' },
 1445:         no_write => { type => SCALAR, optional => 1 },
 1446:     }
 1447: 
 1448: =head4 output
 1449: 
 1450:     {
 1451:         type => HASHREF,
 1452:         keys => {
 1453:             '*' => {
 1454:                 type => HASHREF,
 1455:                 keys => {
 1456:                     function  => { type => SCALAR  },
 1457:                     module    => { type => SCALAR  },
 1458:                     input     => { type => HASHREF },
 1459:                     output    => { type => HASHREF },
 1460:                     _analysis => { type => HASHREF },
 1461:                 },
 1462:             },
 1463:         },
 1464:     }
 1465: 
 1466: =cut
 1467:

 1702: # --------------------------------------------------
 1703: # _delegated_method
 1704: #
 1705: # Purpose:    Detect a routine whose whole body
 1706: #             forwards its arguments to another
 1707: #             method of the same object.
 1708: #
 1709: # Entry:      $code - the subroutine's source text.
 1710: #
 1711: # Exit:       Returns the name of the method it
 1712: #             forwards to, or undef when the routine
 1713: #             does anything else.
 1714: #
 1715: # Side effects: None.
 1716: #
 1717: # Notes:      Only the bare `return $self->other(\@_)`
 1718: #             shape counts. Such a forwarder has
 1719: #             exactly the target's input contract,
 1720: #             which extract_all() copies so that the
 1721: #             harness passes the right arguments
 1722: #             (CGI::Info::get_cookie -> cookie).
 1723: # --------------------------------------------------
 1724: sub _delegated_method {
 1725: 	my ($self, $code) = @_;
 1726: 
 1727: 	return unless defined($code);
 1728: 
 1729: 	(my $body = $code) =~ s/\A\s*sub\s+\w+\s*//;
 1730: 	$body =~ s/\s+/ /g;
 1731: 
 1732: 	return $body =~ /\A\{ (?:my \$self = shift; )?return \$self->(\w+)\(\s*\\\@_\s*\); \}\z/ ? $1 : undef;
 1733: }

 6896: 	} elsif (ref($default) eq 'ARRAY') {
 6897: 		return 'arrayref';

 6899: 		return 'integer';
 6900: 	} elsif ($default =~ /^-?\d+\.\d+$/) {
 6901: 		return 'number';
 6902: 	} elsif ($default eq '1' || $default eq '0') {
 6903: 		return 'boolean';
 6904: 	} else {
 6905: 		return 'string';
 6906: 	}

 7742: # --------------------------------------------------
 7743: # _argument_style
 7744: #
 7745: # Purpose:    Classify how a subroutine reads its arguments.
 7746: #
 7747: # Entry:      $code - the subroutine body as source text.
 7748: #
 7749: # Exit:       'named' when the body reads an argument hash, 'positional'
 7750: #             when it reads an argument by position, '' when neither shows.
 7751: #
 7752: # Notes:      $self->{...} is object state, not an argument, so it is removed
 7753: #             before looking for hash dereferences.  '' is not 'positional':
 7754: #             a wrapper that only forwards @_ proves nothing, so it keeps the
 7755: #             named style these heuristics started from.  An invocant shift
 7756: #             is not an argument.
 7757: #
 7758: # Side effects: None.
 7759: # --------------------------------------------------
 7760: sub _argument_style {
 7761: 	my ($self, $code) = @_;
 7762: 
 7763: 	return '' unless defined $code;
 7764: 
 7765: 	my $body = $code;
 7766: 	$body =~ s/\$self\s*->\s*\{[^}]*\}//g;
 7767: 
 7768: 	return 'named' if($body =~ /\bmy\s+%\w+\s*=[^;]*\@_/s);
 7769: 	return 'named' if($body =~ /\bmy\s*\(\s*[^)]*%\w+\s*\)\s*=\s*\@_/s);
 7770: 	return 'named' if($body =~ /\$_\s*\[\s*\d+\s*\]\s*(?:->\s*)?\{/);
 7771: 	return 'named' if($body =~ /\$\w+\s*->\s*\{/);

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 7772: 
 7773: 	return 'positional' if($body =~ /\$_\s*\[\s*\d+\s*\]/);
 7774: 	return 'positional' if($body =~ /\bmy\s+\$(?!self\b|class\b)\w+\s*=\s*shift\b/);
 7775: 	return 'positional' if($body =~ /\bmy\s*\((?!\s*\$(?:self|class)\s*\))[^)]*\)\s*=\s*\@_/s);
 7776: 
 7777: 	return '';
 7778: }

 7815: # _sanitize_parameter_names

 9896: # _detect_instance_method

10199: # _detect_external_object_dependency
10200: #

10269: 		}

10286: #
10287: # Entry:      None (operates on $self->{_document}).
10288: #
10289: # Exit:       Returns the parent class name string,
10290: #             or undef if no parent is found.
10291: #
10292: # Side effects: None.
10293: # --------------------------------------------------
10294: sub _get_parent_class {
●10295 → 10307 → 1031310295: 	my $self = $_[0];
10296: 
10297: 	my $doc = $self->{_document};
10298: 	return unless $doc;
10299: 
10300: 	# Look for use parent statements
10301: 	my $parent_stmt = $doc->find_first(sub {
10302: 		$_[1]->isa('PPI::Statement::Include') &&

10306: 	});

10308: 		my $parent = $1;
10309: 		return $parent;
10310: 	}
10311: 
10312: 	# Look for @ISA assignment
●10313 → 10317 → 1032110313: 	my $isa_stmt = $doc->find_first(sub {
10314: 		$_[1]->isa('PPI::Statement') &&
10315: 		$_[1]->content =~ /our\s+\@ISA\s*=\s*\(\s*['"](\w+(?:::\w+)*)['"]\s*\)/
10316: 	});
10317: 	if ($isa_stmt && $isa_stmt->content =~ /['"](\w+(?:::\w+)*)['"]/) {
10318: 		return $1;
10319: 	}
10320:

10324: # --------------------------------------------------
10325: # _get_class_for_instance_method
10326: #
10327: # Purpose:    Determine which class should be used
10328: #             for object instantiation when testing
10329: #             an instance method, preferring the
10330: #             current package if it has a new()
10331: #             method, falling back to the parent
10332: #             class otherwise.
10333: #
10334: # Entry:      None (operates on $self->{_document}).
10335: #
10336: # Exit:       Returns the package name string to
10337: #             use for instantiation. Returns
10338: #             'UNKNOWN_PACKAGE' if no package
10339: #             statement is found.
10340: #
10341: # Side effects: Stores the package name in
10342: #               $self->{_package_name} if not
10343: #               already set.
10344: # --------------------------------------------------
10345: sub _get_class_for_instance_method {
●10346 → 10360 → 1036510346: 	my $self = $_[0];
10347: 
10348: 	# Get the current package
10349: 	my $doc = $self->{_document};
10350: 	my $package_stmt = $doc->find_first('PPI::Statement::Package');
10351: 	return 'UNKNOWN_PACKAGE' unless $package_stmt;
10352: 	my $package_name = $package_stmt->namespace;
10353: 	$self->{_package_name} //= $package_name;
10354: 
10355: 	# Check if the current package has a 'new' method
10356: 	my $has_new = $doc->find(sub {
10357: 		$_[1]->isa('PPI::Statement::Sub') && $_[1]->name eq 'new'
10358: 	});
10359: 
10360: 	if ($has_new) {
10361: 		return $package_name;
10362: 	}
10363: 
10364: 	# Otherwise, try to get the parent class
10365: 	my $parent = $self->_get_parent_class();
10366: 	return $parent if $parent;
10367: 
10368: 	# Fallback to current package
10369: 	return $package_name;
10370: }

10372: # --------------------------------------------------
10373: # _extract_default_value
10374: #
10375: # Purpose:    Extract a default value for a named
10376: #             parameter from a method body by
10377: #             matching multiple common Perl default
10378: #             assignment idioms.
10379: #
10380: # Entry:      $param - parameter name string.
10381: #             $code  - method body source string.
10382: #
10383: # Exit:       Returns the cleaned default value
10384: #             scalar on success, or undef if no
10385: #             default assignment pattern is found.
10386: #
10387: # Side effects: None.
10388: #
10389: # Notes:      Eight patterns are tried in order:
10390: #             ||, //=, defined ternary, unless
10391: #             defined, ||=, //, multi-line if
10392: #             !defined, unless defined block.
10393: #             Comment lines are stripped from the
10394: #             code before matching to avoid false
10395: #             positives. Delegates to
10396: #             _clean_default_value for value
10397: #             normalisation.
10398: # --------------------------------------------------
10399: sub _extract_default_value {
●10400 → 10412 → 1042010400: 	my ($self, $param, $code) = @_;
10401: 
10402: 	return undef unless $param && $code;
10403: 
10404: 	# Clean up the code for easier pattern matching
10405: 	# Remove comments to avoid false positives
10406: 	my $clean_code = $code;
10407: 	$clean_code =~ s/#.*$//gm;
10408: 	$clean_code =~ s/^\s+|\s+$//g;
10409: 
10410: 	# Pattern 1: $param = $param || 'default_value'
10411: 	# Also handles: $param = $arg || 'default'
10412: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\|\|\s*([^;]+)/) {
10413: 		my $default = $1;
10414: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10415: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10416: 		return $default if defined $default;
10417: 	}
10418: 
10419: 	# Pattern 2: $param //= 'default_value'
●10420 → 10420 → 1042910420: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*\/\/=\s*([^;]+)/) {
10421: 		my $default = $1;
10422: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10423: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10424: 		return $default if defined $default;
10425: 	}
10426: 
10427: 	# Pattern 3: $param = defined $param ? $param : 'default'
10428: 	# Also handles: $param = defined $arg ? $arg : 'default'
●10429 → 10429 → 1043710429: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*defined\s+(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\?\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*:\s*([^;]+)/) {
10430: 		my $default = $1;
10431: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10432: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10433: 		return $default if defined $default;
10434: 	}
10435: 
10436: 	# Pattern 4: $param = 'default' unless defined $param;
●10437 → 10437 → 1044410437: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+?)\s+unless\s+defined\s+(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)/) {
10438: 		my $default = $1;
10439: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10440: 		return $default if defined $default;
10441: 	}
10442: 
10443: 	# Pattern 5: $param ||= 'default'
●10444 → 10444 → 1045210444: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*\|\|=\s*([^;]+)/) {
10445: 		my $default = $1;
10446: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10447: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10448: 		return $default if defined $default;
10449: 	}
10450: 
10451: 	# Pattern 6: $param = $arg // 'default'
●10452 → 10452 → 1046010452: 	if ($clean_code =~ /\$$param\s*=\s*(?:\$$param|\$[a-zA-Z_]\w*)\s*\/\/\s*([^;]+)/) {
10453: 		my $default = $1;
10454: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10455: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10456: 		return $default if defined $default;
10457: 	}
10458: 
10459: 	# Pattern 7: Multi-line: if (!defined $param) { $param = 'default'; }
●10460 → 10460 → 1046810460: 	if ($clean_code =~ /if\s*\(\s*!defined\s+\$$param\s*\)\s*\{[^}]*\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+)/s) {
10461: 		my $default = $1;
10462: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10463: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10464: 		return $default if defined $default;
10465: 	}
10466: 
10467: 	# Pattern 8: unless (defined $param) { $param = 'default'; }
●10468 → 10468 → 1047510468: 	if ($clean_code =~ /unless\s*\(\s*defined\s+\$$param\s*\)\s*\{[^}]*\$$param\s*=\s*([^;]+)/s) {
10469: 		my $default = $1;
10470: 		$default =~ s/\s*;\s*$//;
10471: 		$default = $self->_clean_default_value($default);
10472: 		return $default if defined $default;
10473: 	}
10474: 
10475: 	return undef;
10476: }

10478: # --------------------------------------------------
10479: # _extract_test_hints
10480: #
10481: # Purpose:    Extract structured test hints from
10482: #             a method's code and schema, including
10483: #             boundary values, invalid inputs, and
10484: #             valid input examples from POD.
10485: #
10486: # Entry:      $method - method hashref.
10487: #             $schema - schema hashref as built so
10488: #                       far by _analyze_method.
10489: #
10490: # Exit:       Returns a hints hashref with keys:
10491: #             boundary_values, invalid_inputs,
10492: #             equivalence_classes, valid_inputs.
10493: #             Keys with empty arrays are deleted
10494: #             before returning.
10495: #
10496: # Side effects: None.
10497: # --------------------------------------------------
10498: sub _extract_test_hints {
●10499 → 10515 → 1051910499: 	my ($self, $method, $schema) = @_;
10500: 
10501: 	my %hints = (
10502: 		boundary_values => [],
10503: 		invalid_inputs => [],
10504: 		equivalence_classes => [],
10505: 		valid_inputs => [],
10506: 	);
10507: 
10508: 	my $code = $method->{body};
10509: 	return {} unless $code;
10510: 
10511: 	$self->_extract_invalid_input_hints($code, \%hints);
10512: 	$self->_extract_boundary_value_hints($code, \%hints);
10513: 
10514: 	# prune empties
10515: 	for my $k (keys %hints) {
10516: 		delete $hints{$k} unless @{$hints{$k}};
10517: 	}
10518: 
10519: 	return \%hints;
10520: }
10521:

10526: #             from a method body by looking for

10539: sub _extract_invalid_input_hints {

10541: 
10542: 	# undef invalid
10543: 	if ($code =~ /defined\s*\(\s*\$/) {
10544: 		push @{ $hints->{invalid_inputs} }, 'undef';
10545: 	}
10546: 
10547: 	# empty string invalid
●10548 → 10548 → 1055310548: 	if ($code =~ /\beq\s*''/ || $code =~ /\blength\s*\(/) {
10549: 		push @{ $hints->{invalid_inputs} }, '';
10550: 	}

●10553 → 10553 → 010553: 	if ($code =~ /\$\w+\s*<\s*0/) {
10554: 		push @{ $hints->{invalid_inputs} }, -1;

10558: # --------------------------------------------------
10559: # _extract_boundary_value_hints
10560: #
10561: # Purpose:    Extract numeric boundary values from
10562: #             comparison operators in a method body,
10563: #             adding both the boundary value and
10564: #             the value one step either side.
10565: #
10566: # Entry:      $code  - method body source string.
10567: #             $hints - hints hashref (modified in
10568: #                      place via boundary_values key).
10569: #
10570: # Exit:       Returns nothing. Appends to and
10571: #             deduplicates $hints->{boundary_values}.
10572: #
10573: # Side effects: None.
10574: # --------------------------------------------------
10575: sub _extract_boundary_value_hints {
●10576 → 10578 → 1059310576: 	my ($self, $code, $hints) = @_;
10577: 
10578: 	while ($code =~ /\$\w+\s*(<=|<|>=|>)\s*(\d+)/g) {
10579: 		my ($op, $n) = ($1, $2);
10580: 
10581: 		if ($op eq '<') {
10582: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n+1;
10583: 		} elsif ($op eq '<=') {
10584: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n+1;
10585: 		} elsif ($op eq '>') {
10586: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n-1;
10587: 		} elsif ($op eq '>=') {
10588: 			push @{ $hints->{boundary_values} }, $n, $n-1;
10589: 		}
10590: 	}
10591: 
10592: 	# Remove duplicates
10593: 	my %seen;
10594: 	$hints->{boundary_values} = [ grep { !$seen{$_}++ } @{ $hints->{boundary_values} } ];
10595: }

10598: # _extract_pod_examples

10693: # --------------------------------------------------
10694: # _clean_default_value
10695: #
10696: # Purpose:    Normalise a raw default value string
10697: #             extracted from code or POD into a
10698: #             clean Perl scalar, handling quoted
10699: #             strings, numeric literals, boolean
10700: #             keywords, empty containers, and
10701: #             undef.
10702: #
10703: # Entry:      $value     - raw value string.
10704: #                          May be undef.
10705: #             $from_code - true if the value was
10706: #                          extracted from source
10707: #                          code (affects escape
10708: #                          sequence handling).
10709: #
10710: # Exit:       Returns the cleaned value:
10711: #               undef   for undef or unparseable
10712: #               {}      for empty hashrefs
10713: #               []      for empty arrayrefs
10714: #               integer for whole numbers
10715: #               float   for decimal numbers
10716: #               1 or 0  for boolean keywords
10717: #               string  for everything else
10718: #

10721: sub _clean_default_value {

10748: 	} elsif ($value =~ /^q[qwx]?\s*([^a-zA-Z0-9\{\[])(.*?)\1$/s) {

10772: 		}

10828: 	}
10829: 
10830: 	return $value;
10831: }
10832: 
10833: # --------------------------------------------------
10834: # _validate_pod_code_agreement
10835: #
10836: # Purpose:    Compare POD parameter documentation
10837: #             against code-inferred parameters and
10838: #             return a list of disagreements when
10839: #             strict_pod mode is enabled.
10840: #
10841: # Entry:      $pod_params  - hashref of parameters
10842: #                            from POD analysis.
10843: #             $code_params - hashref of parameters
10844: #                            from code analysis.
10845: #             $method_name - method name string,
10846: #                            used for context in
10847: #                            error messages.
10848: #
10849: # Exit:       Returns a list of disagreement
10850: #             strings. Returns an empty list if
10851: #             all parameters agree.
10852: #
10853: # Side effects: None.
10854: #
10855: # Notes:      Type mismatches are classified as
10856: #             either 'compatible' (e.g. integer vs
10857: #             number) or 'incompatible' via
10858: #             _types_are_compatible. $self and
10859: #             $class are excluded from undocumented
10860: #             parameter warnings in appropriate
10861: #             context.
10862: # --------------------------------------------------
10863: sub _validate_pod_code_agreement {
●10864 → 10871 → 1094210864: 	my ($self, $pod_params, $code_params, $method_name) = @_;
10865: 
10866: 	my @errors;
10867: 
10868: 	# Get all parameter names from both sources
10869: 	my %all_params = map { $_ => 1 } (keys %$pod_params, keys %$code_params);
10870: 
10871: 	foreach my $param (sort keys %all_params) {
10872: 		my $pod = $pod_params->{$param} || {};
10873: 		my $code = $code_params->{$param} || {};
10874: 
10875: 		# A =head3|4 Input formal spec is authoritative for the parameter's
10876: 		# existence, type, optionality and pattern: heuristic code analysis may
10877: 		# legitimately disagree.  Its bounds are still checked below, because a
10878: 		# formal minimum and a code maximum that do not overlap leave the merged
10879: 		# schema with no valid value.
10880: 		unless(($pod->{_source} // '') eq SOURCE_INPUT_SPEC) {
10881: 			# Check if parameter exists in both
10882: 			if (exists $pod_params->{$param} && !exists $code_params->{$param}) {
10883: 				push @errors, "Parameter '\$$param' documented in POD but not found in code signature";
10884: 				next;
10885: 			}
10886: 
10887: 			if(!exists $pod_params->{$param} && exists $code_params->{$param}) {
10888: 				if($param =~ /^(class|pkg|proto|klass)$/i) {
10889: 					# class invocant, not a user-facing parameter
10890: 					next;
10891: 				}
10892: 				if($param eq 'self') {
10893: 					# instance invocant, not a user-facing parameter
10894: 					next;
10895: 				}
10896: 				push @errors, "Parameter '\$$param' found in code but not documented in POD";
10897: 				next;
10898: 			}
10899: 
10900: 			# Compare types if both exist
10901: 			if ($pod->{type} && $code->{type} && $pod->{type} ne $code->{type}) {
10902: 				if (!$self->_types_are_compatible($pod->{type}, $code->{type})) {
10903: 					push @errors, "Type mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{type}', code suggests '$code->{type}' (incompatible)";
10904: 				} else {
10905: 					push @errors, "Type difference for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{type}', code suggests '$code->{type}' (compatible)";
10906: 				}
10907: 			}
10908: 
10909: 			# Compare optional status if both exist
10910: 			if (exists $pod->{optional} && exists $code->{optional} &&
10911: 				$pod->{optional} != $code->{optional}) {
10912: 				my $pod_status = $pod->{optional} ? 'optional' : 'required';
10913: 				my $code_status = $code->{optional} ? 'optional' : 'required';
10914: 				push @errors, "Optional status mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod_status', code suggests '$code_status'";
10915: 			}
10916: 
10917: 			# Check regex patterns
10918: 			if ($pod->{matches} && $code->{matches} && $pod->{matches} ne $code->{matches}) {
10919: 				push @errors, "Pattern mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{matches}', code suggests '$code->{matches}'";
10920: 			}
10921: 		}
10922: 
10923: 		# Check constraints (min/max), including a formal spec's bounds.
10924: 		if (defined $pod->{min} && defined $code->{min} && $pod->{min} != $code->{min}) {
10925: 			push @errors, "Min constraint mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{min}', code suggests '$code->{min}'";
10926: 		}
10927: 
10928: 		if (defined $pod->{max} && defined $code->{max} && $pod->{max} != $code->{max}) {
10929: 			push @errors, "Max constraint mismatch for '\$$param': POD says '$pod->{max}', code suggests '$code->{max}'";
10930: 		}
10931: 
10932: 		if (defined $pod->{min} && defined $code->{max} && $pod->{min} > $code->{max}) {
10933: 			push @errors, "Constraint contradiction for '\$$param': POD says min '$pod->{min}', code suggests max '$code->{max}' (no valid value)";
10934: 		}
10935: 
10936: 		if (defined $code->{min} && defined $pod->{max} && $code->{min} > $pod->{max}) {
10937: 			push @errors, "Constraint contradiction for '\$$param': code suggests min '$code->{min}', POD says max '$pod->{max}' (no valid value)";
10938: 		}
10939: 	}
10940: 
10941: 	# Return errors (empty array if no errors)
10942: 	return @errors;
10943: }
10944: 
10945: # --------------------------------------------------
10946: # _validate_strictness_level
10947: #
10948: # Purpose:    Validate and normalise the strict_pod
10949: #             option value accepted by new() into
10950: #             an integer level: 0 (off), 1 (warn),
10951: #             or 2 (fatal).
10952: #
10953: # Entry:      $val - the raw value passed to
10954: #                    strict_pod in new(). May be
10955: #                    undef, a number, or a string.
10956: #
10957: # Exit:       Returns 0, 1, or 2.
10958: #             Croaks if the value is not recognised.
10959: #
10960: # Side effects: None.
10961: # --------------------------------------------------
10962: sub _validate_strictness_level {
10963: 	my $val = $_[0];
10964: 
10965: 	return 0 unless defined $val;
10966: 
10967: 	# Numeric
10968: 	return 0 if $val =~ /^(0|off|none)$/i;
10969: 	return 1 if $val =~ /^(1|warn|warning)$/i;
10970: 	return 2 if $val =~ /^(2|fatal|die|error)$/i;
10971: 
10972: 	croak("Invalid value for --strict-pod: '$val' (use off|warn|fatal)");
10973: }
10974: 
10975: # --------------------------------------------------
10976: # _types_are_compatible
10977: #
10978: # Purpose:    Determine whether two type strings
10979: #             are compatible for POD/code agreement
10980: #             checking, allowing semantically
10981: #             equivalent types (e.g. 'integer' and
10982: #             'number') to coexist without
10983: #             triggering a strict POD warning.
10984: #
10985: # Entry:      $pod_type  - type string from POD.
10986: #             $code_type - type string from code.
10987: #
10988: # Exit:       Returns 1 if compatible, 0 otherwise.
10989: #
10990: # Side effects: None.
10991: # --------------------------------------------------
10992: sub _types_are_compatible {
●10993 → 11009 → 1101410993: 	my ($self, $pod_type, $code_type) = @_;
10994: 
10995: 	# Exact match is always compatible
10996: 	return 1 if $pod_type eq $code_type;
10997: 
10998: 	# Define compatibility matrix
10999: 	my %compatible_types = (
11000: 		'integer' => ['number', 'scalar'],
11001: 		'number' => ['scalar'],
11002: 		'string' => ['scalar'],
11003: 		'scalar' => ['string', 'integer', 'number'],
11004: 		'arrayref' => ['array'],
11005: 		'hashref' => ['hash'],
11006: 	);
11007: 
11008: 	# Check if code_type is compatible with pod_type
11009: 	if (my $allowed = $compatible_types{$pod_type}) {
11010: 		return grep { $_ eq $code_type } @$allowed;
11011: 	}
11012: 
11013: 	# Check if pod_type is compatible with code_type
●11014 → 11014 → 1101811014: 	if (my $allowed = $compatible_types{$code_type}) {
11015: 		return grep { $_ eq $pod_type } @$allowed;
11016: 	}
11017: 
11018: 	return 0;	# Not compatible
11019: }
11020: 
11021: =head2 generate_pod_validation_report
11022: 
11023: Generate a human-readable report of all POD/code disagreements found
11024: across a set of extracted schemas.
11025: 
11026:     my $schemas = $extractor->extract_all(no_write => 1);
11027:     my $report  = $extractor->generate_pod_validation_report($schemas);
11028:     print $report;
11029: 
11030: =head3 Arguments
11031: 
11032: =over 4
11033: 
11034: =item * C<$schemas>
11035: 
11036: A hashref of method name to schema hashref as returned by
11037: C<extract_all>. Required.
11038: 
11039: =back
11040: 
11041: =head3 Returns
11042: 
11043: A string containing the full validation report, or a single line
11044: confirming all methods passed if no disagreements were found.
11045: 
11046: =head3 Side effects
11047: 
11048: None.
11049: 
11050: =head3 Notes
11051: 
11052: Only methods whose schemas contain a C<_pod_validation_errors> key
11053: (populated when C<strict_pod> is 1 or 2) appear in the report. If
11054: C<strict_pod> was 0 when C<extract_all> was called, this method will
11055: always return the all-passed message.
11056: 
11057: =head3 API specification
11058: 
11059: =head4 input
11060: 
11061:     {
11062:         self    => { type => OBJECT,  isa => 'App::Test::Generator::SchemaExtractor' },
11063:         schemas => { type => HASHREF },
11064:     }
11065: 
11066: =head4 output
11067: 
11068:     { type => SCALAR }
11069: 
11070: =cut
11071: 
11072: sub generate_pod_validation_report {
●11073 → 11076 → 1108811073: 	my ($self, $schemas) = @_;
11074: 
11075: 	my @reports;
11076: 	foreach my $method_name (sort keys %$schemas) {
11077: 		my $schema = $schemas->{$method_name};
11078: 
11079: 		if (my $errors = $schema->{_pod_validation_errors}) {
11080: 			push @reports, "Method: $method_name";
11081: 			push @reports, "  Severity: " . ($schema->{_pod_disagreement} ? 'warning' : 'fatal');
11082: 			push @reports, "  Errors:";
11083: 			push @reports, map { "    - $_" } @$errors;
11084: 			push @reports, '';
11085: 		}
11086: 	}
11087: 
●11088 → 11088 → 011088: 	if (@reports) {
11089: 		return join("\n", "POD/Code Validation Report:", '=' x 40, '', @reports);
11090: 	} else {
11091: 		return 'POD/Code Validation: All methods passed consistency checks.';
11092: 	}
11093: }
11094: 
11095: =head2 _log
11096: 
11097: Log a message if verbose mode is on.
11098: 
11099: =cut
11100: 
11101: sub _log {
11102: 	my($self, $msg) = @_;
11103: 
11104: 	print "$msg\n" if $self->{verbose};
11105: }
11106: 
11107: =head1 NOTES
11108: 
11109: C<SchemaExtractor> uses heuristic analysis of Perl source and POD to infer
11110: parameter types and constraints. Inference accuracy improves with
11111: well-documented modules; C<=head3 Input> / C<=head4 Input> formal specs are
11112: parsed at highest priority and override all heuristics.
11113: 
11114: A lone name in a C<=head4 input> block is treated as the first positional
11115: argument when the body reads a positional argument (C<< $_[0] >>, a list
11116: assignment from C<@_>, or a C<shift>) and does not read an argument hash
11117: (C<my %args = @_>, a hashref dereference, or C<< $_[0]{...} >>); the
11118: documented name is then a label for that argument, as C<name> is for
11119: C<$_[0]> in L<Lingua::EN::NameCase/nc>.  Two or more names, and a body that
11120: only forwards C<@_>, remain named arguments.
11121: 
11122: The output is always a best-effort schema suitable as a starting template;
11123: review and augment the generated YAML before using it as a definitive
11124: specification. Pass it to L<App::Test::Generator> to generate fuzz harnesses.
11125: 
11126: =head1 TODO
11127: 
11128: Extend C<=head4 Input> parsing to cover the C<enum>/C<memberof> constraint
11129: synonym (union types, e.g. C<scalar | scalarref>, are already handled by
11130: C<_map_formal_input_type>).
11131: 
11132: =head1 SEE ALSO
11133: 
11134: =over 4
11135: 
11136: =item * L<App::Test::Generator> - Generate fuzz and corpus-driven test harnesses
11137: 
11138: Output from this module serves as input to that module.
11139: So with well-documented code, you can automatically create your tests.
11140: 
11141: =item * L<App::Test::Generator::Template> - Template of the file of tests created by C<App::Test::Generator>
11142: 
11143: =back
